# Policy v2 study: results

**Status: pre-registered study, completed.** This page reports the [analysis plan](analysis-plan.md) as it was frozen, applied to the one attempt that ran. The plan was frozen at the merge of the pull request that added it. That pull request's number, merge commit and merge time are recorded in [`evidence/reproduction.json`](evidence/reproduction.json), with the tooling commit, container image and UTC start and end of every arm, and a contract test checks that the merge commit precedes the commit each arm ran. Every gate passed, so the v1 reference is the released v1.0.0 records and no result here is exploratory. No deviation from the plan was recorded.

The study reran the released evaluation cohort, `common_valid_tokens` = 344 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.common_valid_tokens -->, in five arms:

- **A**, the released controller rerun (policy v1, released keying, finite-difference velocity);
- **B**, policy v2: the AEB may select only a body that the unchanged straight-line rollout along the ego's current heading predicts will overlap the ego's margin-inflated footprint within the rollout horizon, and whose centre is not behind the ego;
- **C**, policy v1 with a constant-velocity Kalman velocity estimate;
- **D**, policy v2 with that estimate;
- **E**, policy v2 with the error channels' random draws keyed independently of every severity.

A contrast is written as one arm minus another in the same cell, for example B − A in `oracle_aeb`. The "full coalition" is the cell with all four error channels at medium, `coalition-dropout+localization_shape+latency+track_instability`. Braking share is the share of simulated time in partial or full braking, including time held in braking at standstill; it is not an activation rate. An early end is a run whose simulated time ends short of the full horizon without a counted collision, because the logged route or the log's data ended first; it does not mean that the ego stopped. Induced and avoided collisions are counted against the same token's `no_aeb` run: induced when `no_aeb` has no counted collision and the cell has one, avoided the other way round.

Every number is written as `` `metric_key` = value `` and bound by a claim marker to [`claims.yaml`](claims.yaml), and the attribution audit checks each one against the evidence in [`evidence/`](evidence/). A shortened value is rounded half to even on its decimal text, and the audit recomputes it from its claim; the exact values are in the evidence. A percentile interval is written from its low to its high bound, with its confidence.

## Answer

**Q1: Partial support.** Limiting the AEB's targets to bodies on a predicted collision course that are not behind the ego lowered the share of simulated time spent braking under oracle perception from `braking_share` = 0.634 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.a-v1-replication.oracle_aeb.braking_share; rounded: 3 --> to `braking_share` = 0.158 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.b-v2-gated.oracle_aeb.braking_share; rounded: 3 -->, a B − A difference of `estimate` = -0.476 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h1.braking_share.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-estimate; rounded: 3 --> (H1, supported), and with all four error channels at medium from `braking_share` = 0.889 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.a-v1-replication.coalition-dropout-localization_shape-latency-track_instability.braking_share; rounded: 3 --> to `braking_share` = 0.809 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.b-v2-gated.coalition-dropout-localization_shape-latency-track_instability.braking_share; rounded: 3 -->, a difference of `estimate` = -0.080 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h2.braking_share.plus-b-v2-gated-coalition-dropout-localization_shape-latency-track_instability.minus-a-v1-replication-coalition-dropout-localization_shape-latency-track_instability.contrast-estimate; rounded: 3 --> (H2, supported). Support requires both reductions to reach ten percentage points of simulated time, and H2's does not, so the label is Partial support.
The label says where the released braking came from, not that the controller improved: under oracle perception most of the braking time went away once only such bodies could be selected, while with all four channels at medium most of it remained.
This study does not detect a change in counted collisions under oracle perception. For H5 (`oracle_aeb`, B − A on `collision_indicator`) the estimate is `estimate` = 0.0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-estimate -->, with sign-flip `p_value` = 1.0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-p_value --> and an interval from `low` = -0.0285 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-interval-low; rounded: 4 --> to `high` = 0.0299 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-interval-high; rounded: 4 --> at `confidence` = 0.95 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-interval-confidence -->, and Holm classifies it not established. The label carries no qualifier: the qualifier needs H5's unadjusted sign-flip p below the five-percent level and an estimate above zero, and neither holds. Counted collision records are `collisions` = 39 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.a-v1-replication.oracle_aeb.collisions --> under A and `collisions` = 39 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.b-v2-gated.oracle_aeb.collisions --> under B, in `successes` = 13 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-clopper_pearson-1-successes --> and `successes` = 13 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-clopper_pearson-0-successes --> of `trials` = 344 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-clopper_pearson-0-trials --> tokens (Clopper-Pearson `low` = 0.0203 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h5.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-clopper_pearson-1-low; These collision contrasts are descriptive and outside the Q2 family.

**Q3 (keying).** The ratio of the cluster-bootstrap standard deviations of the braking-share contrast full coalition − `localization_shape-medium`, under B to that under E, is `ratio` = 1.11 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.q3_ratio.ratio; rounded: 2 -->. The implementation check rejected none of its ten contrasts, so no audit of the keying code was triggered.

## Primary result

Every primary contrast is B − A, with Holm over the five hypotheses. Three intervals are given for each: simultaneous, at the confidence of its Holm step, and at the level of the secondary results. None of them decides a hypothesis; the Holm procedure on the p-values does. The rows below each hypothesis are its sensitivity analyses, which enter no decision.

| | Contrast | Estimate | p | Simultaneous interval | Holm-step interval | Interval | Holm |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

H3 is supported: under oracle perception, gating lowered the not-at-fault contact rate from `not_at_fault_contact_rate` = 73.5 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.a-v1-replication.oracle_aeb.not_at_fault_contact_rate; rounded: 1 --> to `not_at_fault_contact_rate` = 26.7 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.b-v2-gated.oracle_aeb.not_at_fault_contact_rate; rounded: 1 --> contacts per thousand simulated seconds, a B − A difference of `estimate` = -46.7 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.hypotheses.h3.not_at_fault_contact_rate.plus-b-v2-gated-oracle_aeb.minus-a-v1-replication-oracle_aeb.contrast-estimate; rounded: 1 -->. Excluded contacts and counted collisions are coupled by the stopped-ego rule, so less braking can move contacts from the excluded class into counted collisions.

H4 and H5 are stated with the answer above, in the wording the plan prescribes. Both collision contrasts are decided by the exact sign-flip test over logs, which the plan uses because a bootstrap p-value is too small for sparse paired events. For H4 the three intervals of the primary row exclude zero, but the sign-flip p-value is above the threshold of its Holm step, so H4 is not established.

## Secondary results

These contrasts form no family of tests. Each interval is a two-sided percentile interval at `confidence` = 0.95 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.secondary.other_cells.collision_indicator.plus-b-v2-gated-coalition-none.minus-a-v1-replication-coalition-none.contrast-interval-confidence -->; each p-value is the sign-flip test over logs for `collision_indicator` and the cluster bootstrap otherwise.

### How much of the error pattern survives gating

Each row is a difference in differences: (error cell − `coalition-none`) under B minus the same under A.

**Pre-declared cost check.** In `coalition-none` and the dropout, latency and track instability cells, the filter can only smooth the logged boxes' own jitter and adds lag, so C − A on `collision_indicator` was expected at or above zero and the matched onset delay later under C. The collision estimates are `estimate` = 0.0087 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.descriptive_contrasts.velocity_by_cell.collision_indicator.plus-c-v1-kalman-coalition-none.minus-a-v1-replication-coalition-none.contrast-estimate; rounded: 4 --> in `coalition-none`, `estimate` = 0.0126 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.descriptive_contrasts.velocity_by_cell.collision_indicator.plus-c-v1-kalman-dropout-medium.minus-a-v1-replication-dropout-medium.contrast-estimate; rounded: 4 --> with dropout, `estimate` = 0.0087 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.descriptive_contrasts.velocity_by_cell.collision_indicator.plus-c-v1-kalman-latency-medium.minus-a-v1-replication-latency-medium.contrast-estimate; rounded: 4 --> with latency and `estimate` = 0.0048 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.descriptive_contrasts.velocity_by_cell.collision_indicator.plus-c-v1-kalman-track_instability-medium.minus-a-v1-replication-track_instability-medium.contrast-estimate; rounded: 4 --> with track instability: each at or above zero, each with an interval that contains zero (table below).

### Q3: keying

The answer is the ratio of the cluster-bootstrap standard deviations of the braking-share contrast full coalition − `localization_shape-medium`: `sd_b` = 0.00269 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.q3_ratio.sd_b; rounded: 5 --> under B, `sd_e` = 0.00242 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.q3_ratio.sd_e; rounded: 5 --> under E, a ratio of `ratio` = 1.11 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.q3_ratio.ratio; rounded: 2 -->. Under E the two cells share their localization draws; E's `localization_shape-medium` is B's, because the keying changes no draw in a cell without dropout. The ratio is descriptive.

**Implementation check.** E − B in `dropout-medium` and the full coalition, on five outcomes, with Holm over the ten contrasts. Both keyings draw from the same distributions, so a rejection would have triggered an audit of the keying code rather than be read as an effect. None was rejected.

## Levels by arm and cell (descriptive)

Overall values over the whole cohort; the evidence also holds them per scenario family, with maximum deceleration, stop distances, collision speeds and matched onset delays. Missed and false interventions are measured against each arm's own oracle, and arms with different policies have different oracles, so they are not compared across policies and are left to the evidence.

Two cells have no counted collision in any token and replicate: A in `localization_shape-medium` and A in the full coalition. The analysis plan gives their Clopper-Pearson bounds per scenario family as well, at `confidence` = 0.95 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.levels.a-v1-replication.localization_shape-medium.lead_or_stopping.collision_clopper_pearson-confidence -->. Within a family n is smaller, so the bound is wider; like every Clopper-Pearson bound on this page, its n counts tokens, which are correlated within logs, so it is too narrow.

## What this does not show

- **This is a simulation of non-reactive logged traffic; it says nothing about real-world AEB performance.** It makes no regulatory or standards-compliance claim.
- **Selection coupling.** Every cohort token was admitted because at least one oracle body passed the collision-course predicate that is policy v2's first condition, against the logged ego within the first four seconds (`src/aebrisk/cohort/prefilter.py`). The cohort therefore under-represents threats that a straight-line, current-heading rollout does not predict. H5 cannot estimate how often the gate misses such threats outside this cohort. The protocol comment describes the prefilter corridor as swept along the expert route. The code, which the analysis plan follows, uses the straight-line rollout along the current heading.
- **The velocity leak.** Under localization error the rollout uses a velocity taken from finite differences of noisy positions, so bodies beside the path, and bodies alongside whose centre is ahead, can still be predicted to overlap on many steps; a leak on some steps can hold the AEB in braking, because release needs several clear steps. This is consistent with H2's small reduction and with the much larger reductions of the Kalman arms in those cells.
- **Not established is not no effect.** H4 and H5 are decided on few collision events by an exact test over logs, which can detect only large differences. Neither result shows that gating leaves counted collisions unchanged. Under the Kalman estimate with policy v1 (C − A) and under the combined remedy (D − A), the cells with localization error, which have no counted collision under the released controller, have some.
- **Counted collisions and excluded contacts are coupled.** A contact while the ego is stopped, or with a faster body from behind, is excluded, so less braking can move contacts into counted collisions. Braking share also counts time held in braking at standstill.
- **Policy v2 is still an imperfect controller.** Its rollout is straight and along the current heading, not along the route, so curves can be judged wrongly by v1 and v2 alike; among candidates it still selects by required deceleration; release still reads only the selected candidate; and the nominal controller still does not slow for other road users.
- **One Kalman filter.** The Kalman arms run one fixed parameter set, which lags decelerating targets, and share two oracle elements with the released estimator: initialisation with the reported velocity and fusion of detections hidden by dropout. Their results describe this filter, not the best tracker.
- **Clustering beyond the log.** Intervals resample logs within families; the logs are segments of fewer drives, and the drive-level sensitivity rows show how much the intervals and the H4 and H5 p-values move.

## How the numbers were produced and checked

The arms ran one after another in the pinned container, on CPU, from the study tooling commit, each into its own directory of one attempt. The operator log, [`evidence/operator-log.txt`](evidence/operator-log.txt), records every command with its UTC time, from the environment build to the commands below; its dataset root is written as `$NUPLAN_DATA_ROOT`. The per-token records stay outside Git under the nuPlan licence. The evidence and the claims registry were written by these commands, run from the repository root in the same image with the released records and the study's records mounted read-only:

```bash
docker compose run --rm dev uv run --frozen aeb-risk study analyse --study configs/experiments/aeb_policy_v2_study.yaml --arms-root artifacts/formal/aeb_policy_v2/attempt-1 --released-root artifacts/formal/nuplan_aeb_v2 --evidence-dir docs/evidence/nuplan_aeb_v2 --gates artifacts/formal/aeb_policy_v2/attempt-1.gates.json --manifest docs/evidence/nuplan_aeb_v2/cohort/evaluation.json --output artifacts/studies/aeb_policy_v2/summary.json
docker compose run --rm dev uv run --frozen aeb-risk study evidence --part study --summary artifacts/studies/aeb_policy_v2/summary.json --gates artifacts/formal/aeb_policy_v2/attempt-1.gates.json --arms-root artifacts/formal/aeb_policy_v2/attempt-1 --g0 artifacts/formal/aeb_policy_v2/g0.json --preregistration-pr 5 --preregistration-commit 42b36453d709c1765f6c721b260a26ad790b44fb --preregistration-merged-at 2026-09-26T06:40:03Z --output-dir docs/studies/aeb-policy-v2/evidence
docker compose run --rm dev uv run --frozen aeb-risk study claims --part study --evidence-dir docs/studies/aeb-policy-v2/evidence --output docs/studies/aeb-policy-v2/claims.yaml
```

Every gate passed before any contrast was computed:

- **G0, before the formal run.** The study tooling merged with full branch coverage, its CI run is recorded, and a pilot of all five arms on the smoke manifest, whose tokens are not in the cohort, had every token valid. The record is `g0` in [`evidence/reproduction.json`](evidence/reproduction.json).
- **G1, integrity of every arm.** `documents` = 12384 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.documents -->, `documents_missing` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.documents_missing -->, `documents_invalid` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.documents_invalid -->, `invalid_tokens` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.invalid_tokens -->, `tokens_not_listed` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.tokens_not_listed -->, `unexpected_files` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.unexpected_files -->, `run_complete_problems` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.run_complete_problems -->, `run_context_problems` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.run_context_problems -->, `distinct_commits` = 1 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.distinct_commits -->, `distinct_container_digests` = 1 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g1.distinct_container_digests -->.
  No arm lists a token outside the released cohort manifest.
- **G2, v1 replication.** Documents of arm A that differ from the released document for the same cell and token: `coalition_dropout_localization_shape_latency_track_instability` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.coalition-dropout-localization_shape-latency-track_instability -->, `coalition_none` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.coalition-none -->, `dropout_medium` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.dropout-medium -->, `latency_medium` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.latency-medium -->, `localization_shape_medium` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.localization_shape-medium -->, `no_aeb` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.no_aeb -->, `oracle_aeb` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.oracle_aeb -->, `track_instability_medium` = 0 <!-- claim: p3.study.policy-v2.gates.g2.track_instability-medium -->.

The claims registry audits clean against the evidence, and this page passes the attribution audit against it; the Pages build runs both, and `tests/contract/test_policy_v2_study_results.py` also pins the summary's hash, derives the evidence from it byte for byte, rebuilds the registry, checks that no scenario token appears in any published file, and checks that the pre-registration merge commit is an ancestor of every arm's tooling commit:

```bash
docker compose run --rm dev uv run --frozen aeb-risk audit-claims --claims docs/studies/aeb-policy-v2/claims.yaml
docker compose run --rm dev uv run --frozen python .agents/skills/auditing-aeb-error-attribution/scripts/validate_attribution.py --claims docs/studies/aeb-policy-v2/claims.yaml --repo-root . --document docs/studies/aeb-policy-v2/results.md
docker compose run --rm dev uv run --frozen python -m pytest tests/contract/test_policy_v2_study_results.py
```
